Implement a creative-work mode in megaplan, generalizing today's `--mode joke` into a `--mode creative --form <form>` system with a real provocation engine.

## The provocation engine

The brief fires a small set of provocations at the executor — adversarial prompts that demand an act on the work, not introspection. Three vectors:

- **Cuts** — strip / expose / kill. Example: "Cut the line you'd fight hardest to keep. If the piece is worse, you were right; if it's the same, you killed a darling."
- **Forces** — perform a transformation on the piece. Example: "Halve it. Write the line that survives."
- **Sparks** — the brief itself supplies foreign material the maker reacts to. This is the new vector vs joke mode today. Sub-types:
  - Hostile gifts (an image, line, or detail invented in the brief and asserted to belong)
  - Substitutions ("what if the protagonist were the antagonist of this same scene")
  - Inversions ("the version of this that's a confession, not a performance")
  - Forced transplants (steal a structure from a different form: tarot spread, recipe, courtroom transcript)
  - Borrowed moves (steal one specific move from a master in this form, named)
  - Hostile claims ("this piece is afraid of the thing it's actually about" — refute or absorb)
  - Generative constraints (half the words forbidden, no character speaks, must rhyme)
  - Sensory escalation (what does it smell like / sound like in the next room)
  - Dares (name the move that would get you booed; try it)

Default firing pattern: **one cut, one force, one spark per pass.** Three is plenty; five is a lecture. The prep phase chooses the specific provocations targeted at the draft state — over-explained drafts get cuts on the unsaid; safe drafts get risk/dare sparks; bloated drafts get force-halve; replaceable-feeling drafts get borrowed-move + "who could have made this."

## The stance

Every executor task output includes a stance field: in the maker's authentic voice, naming the provocation engaged, the angle taken, and what changed. Hard constraints to prevent LLM-flavor sand:

- ≤50 words
- First person
- Names the specific provocation engaged
- No hedging verbs (considered / tried / attempted / sought to)
- Must take a position someone could disagree with

A sub-provocation fires at the stance itself: "Read it aloud. If it's safe, the work is too. Rewrite the part that would embarrass you to say to a stranger."

Stance lives in per-task receipts and is bubbled into a "director's notes" sidecar artifact. NOT inline in the final piece — the artifact must stand alone.

## Robustness mapping

Creative work has a different process curve than code: it peaks and then declines into over-sanded mush. Robustness should mean *different angles, harder targets, more diverse provocateur voices* — not just more cycles.

- **Light** — single pass, one cut + one spark (skip the force), stance ≤2 sentences, no critique cycle. For sketches and throwaways. Failure mode protected against: over-process killing a quick instinct.
- **Standard** — one critique/revise cycle. Full triad on prep. Critique fires *different* provocations from a different angle (no relitigating addressed material). Revise stance must explicitly contrast the prior stance.
- **Robust** — multiple cycles with *different provocateur voices*: formalist (structure/economy), iconoclast (risk/lie/dare), audience (stakes/specificity for one named person). Review evaluates full scene canvas + stance authenticity + alignment.

## Executor stop-affordance

Let the maker signal "the next pass would damage the piece" with a stance defending why. Code mode shouldn't have this (more review = safer); creative must. Without it, robust mode reliably over-edits.

## Generalize joke → creative-work

- New mode: `--mode creative --form <form>` with joke as the first registered form
- Existing `--mode joke` continues to work as an alias for `--mode creative --form joke` — no breakage of existing plans
- Prove the abstraction by adding a second form: prep phase picks the cheapest validation form (poem or short ad copy)
- Form-specific prompt fragments inject into shared scaffolds; we do NOT fork five new prompt files per form

## Design branches the prep phase should resolve

The following are real branches with arguments either way — settle them in prep, not in code:

1. Stance field shape: single string vs structured (`challenge_engaged` / `angle_taken` / `what_changed`)
2. Director's notes destination: separate sidecar file, YAML frontmatter on the artifact, or only in receipts
3. Stop-affordance signal: new outcome status, stance flag, or separate halt-task type
4. Provocateur voices in robust mode: fixed roster (formalist / iconoclast / audience) vs model-chosen per cycle
5. Robustness → provocation-count mapping: formal table vs executor discretion within bounds
6. Whether to retire `--primary-criterion` as joke-only and lift it to creative-work-wide, or keep both surfaces

## Out of scope

- New forms beyond the second validation form
- Non-text creative outputs (audio/visual)
- Team-of-makers workflows
- Backwards-compat for any pre-v0.21 joke-mode plan files (current plans untouched; new plans use new schema)

## Primary criterion (for prep guidance)

Sharpest provocation system that survives over-process. The system should produce work that has *a stance*, took risks, made specific choices, and refused something — and should produce that work without grinding it into nervous mush via too many cycles. If the system is "more rigorous" but the output is more sanded, we have failed. The stance authenticity check is the single most load-bearing piece — it is what converts the system from "external pressure" into "training the maker as their own provocateur."

User notes and answers:
- Creative-light workflow flag cluster (FLAG-005, issue_hints, scope, callers, correctness, all_locations) all surface the same single issue: handle_finalize() requires STATE_GATED but the v3 plan proposes surfacing finalize from STATE_PLANNED for creative-light. Resolution at implementation time: route creative-light through gate (plan->gate->finalize) like other modes, with a creative-light-specific gate that auto-passes (no critique cycle), rather than skipping gate entirely. This preserves the state machine contract and the brief's intent (single-pass, no critique cycle for creative-light). Executor should pick the cheapest of: (a) creative-light gate auto-pass, or (b) loosening handle_finalize to accept STATE_PLANNED when mode=creative+robustness=light. Recurring critique = same fault, not new fault.

        # Implementation Plan: Creative-Work Mode + Provocation Engine

## Overview

Generalize today's `--mode joke` into `--mode creative --form <form>` with a registered-form system, replace the static joke "lens" critique with a real **provocation engine** (cuts / forces / sparks), add a per-task **stance** field with hard constraints, route stances into a **director's notes sidecar** assembled at the merge boundary, add an executor **stop-affordance** at the correct vertex (the rework branch), and remap robustness so higher levels mean *different angles and provocateur voices*, not more cycles.

**Root-cause framing.** The previous draft fixed the prompt-builder layer but every other mode-aware surface (`mode in {"doc", "joke"}` and `mode == "joke"`) is duplicated across the codebase. Verified instances (from grep):
- `megaplan/evaluation.py:124`, `megaplan/handlers/init.py:64`, `megaplan/handlers/execute.py:106`, `megaplan/handlers/critique.py:120`, `megaplan/cli.py:307`, `megaplan/handlers/review.py:273`
- `megaplan/execute/core.py:163, 169, 209, 308, 401, 468, 1085`
- `megaplan/execute/timeout.py:49, 70, 138, 299`
- `megaplan/schemas.py:653` (`get_execution_schema_key`)
- `megaplan/workers.py:819`, `megaplan/hermes_worker.py:439`
- `megaplan/prompts/__init__.py:128, 137`, `megaplan/prompts/critique.py:302`, `megaplan/prompts/finalize.py:32`, `megaplan/prompts/planning.py:103`, `megaplan/prompts/review.py:85`

These *all* answer one of two questions: "is this a prose-artifact mode?" or "which form's prompt builder do I use?" The fix is two helpers — `is_creative_mode(state) -> bool` and `creative_form_id(state) -> str | None` — added once, called everywhere. Same shape as the existing `configured_robustness(state)` helper. This is a mechanical sweep, not new logic.

**Workflow override (FLAG-001 / issue_hints-1 / FLAG-005 / issue_hints).** Verified `WORKFLOW` at `megaplan/_core/workflow.py:34-80`: the natural creative path is `STATE_INITIALIZED → prep → STATE_PREPPED → plan → STATE_PLANNED → critique → STATE_CRITIQUED → ... → execute → STATE_EXECUTED → review → STATE_DONE`. `_ROBUSTNESS_OVERRIDES["standard"]` injects `STATE_INITIALIZED → plan` (skipping prep). `_ROBUSTNESS_OVERRIDES["light"]` adds `STATE_INITIALIZED → plan` (skip prep), `STATE_CRITIQUED → revise → STATE_GATED` (skip gate), `STATE_EXECUTED: []` (skip review) — but does NOT override `STATE_PLANNED`, so light *still runs critique*. For creative we need both: standard runs prep AND light skips critique entirely.

**Stop-affordance at the correct vertex (FLAG-002 / issue_hints-2 / correctness-2 / callers).** `_resolve_review_outcome()` at `megaplan/handlers/review.py:149-201` is the right place to halt a `needs_rework` cycle, but its current signature has no `plan_dir`. Verified call sites at `review.py:332-337` and `483-488` pass only `(review_verdict, verdict_count, total_tasks, check_count, total_checks, missing_evidence, robustness, state, issues, criteria=…)`. Step 11 adds `plan_dir: Path` to the signature and updates both call sites.

**Stop-signal data path (correctness-1 / FLAG-004).** Verified `_merge_batch_results()` at `megaplan/execute/core.py:282-330`: for prose modes the merge field tuple is `("status", "executor_notes", "sections_written")`. Without explicit propagation, `stop_signal` and `stance` from `execution_doc.json` task_updates never reach `finalize.json`. Step 5 adds both to the `finalize.json` schema; Step 6 extends the prose-mode `merge_fields` tuple.

**Director's notes idempotency (scope).** Verified `_build_aggregate_execution_payload()` is called at the end of an execute run from `megaplan/execute/core.py:431, 679, 1033`. To avoid double-appending pass entries (once per batch + once at aggregate), Step 12 commits to a single hook: write `directors_notes.json` ONLY from `_build_aggregate_execution_payload()`, never from `_merge_batch_results()`. The merge function still propagates `stance` / `stop_signal` into `finalize.json`; the sidecar reads from the aggregate.

**Joke alias as a thin shim.** `--mode joke` continues to work. At init time it's stored as `mode = "joke"` (preserved verbatim for in-flight plans) AND `form = "joke"` is stamped onto config. The two helpers (`is_creative_mode`, `creative_form_id`) treat `mode in {"creative", "joke"}` as creative and resolve form from `state.config.form` (or `"joke"` when missing).

The whole change is contained inside `megaplan/cli.py`, `megaplan/handlers/`, `megaplan/prompts/`, `megaplan/audits/robustness.py`, `megaplan/schemas.py`, `megaplan/_core/`, `megaplan/execute/core.py`, `megaplan/execute/timeout.py`, `megaplan/workers.py`, `megaplan/hermes_worker.py`, `megaplan/evaluation.py`, plus a new `megaplan/forms/` package and tests under `tests/`.

## Phase 1: Foundation — mode helpers, forms registry, schemas, workflow override

### Step 1: Add mode helpers (`megaplan/_core/modes.py`, re-export from `megaplan/_core/__init__.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Add** `megaplan/_core/modes.py`:
   ```python
   def is_creative_mode(state: PlanState) -> bool:
       mode = state.get("config", {}).get("mode", "code")
       return mode in {"creative", "joke"}

   def creative_form_id(state: PlanState) -> str | None:
       if not is_creative_mode(state):
           return None
       form = state.get("config", {}).get("form")
       if isinstance(form, str) and form.strip():
           return form.strip()
       if state.get("config", {}).get("mode") == "joke":
           return "joke"
       return None

   def is_prose_mode(state: PlanState) -> bool:
       """True for any mode that writes a prose artifact (doc, metaplan-as-doc, creative)."""
       mode = state.get("config", {}).get("mode", "code")
       return mode in {"doc", "joke", "creative"}
   ```
2. **Re-export** from `megaplan/_core/__init__.py` so existing call sites can `from megaplan._core import is_creative_mode, creative_form_id, is_prose_mode`.

### Step 4: Mode-aware workflow override (`megaplan/_core/workflow.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Audit** `megaplan/_core/workflow.py:34-105`. Verified facts:
   - `WORKFLOW[STATE_INITIALIZED] = [Transition("prep", STATE_PREPPED)]` — natural path runs prep.
   - `_ROBUSTNESS_OVERRIDES["standard"]` injects `STATE_INITIALIZED → plan` (skips prep).
   - `_ROBUSTNESS_OVERRIDES["light"]` overrides INITIALIZED/CRITIQUED/EXECUTED but **not STATE_PLANNED**, so light still runs critique inherited from `WORKFLOW`.
2. **Add** a `_resolve_overrides(robustness: str, *, creative: bool) -> dict[str, list[Transition]]` helper inside workflow.py:
   - For `creative=False`: return today's `_ROBUSTNESS_OVERRIDES[robustness]` after walking `_ROBUSTNESS_WORKFLOW_LEVELS` (no behavior change).
   - For `creative=True`:
     - **standard** → return `{}` (drop the standard override that skips prep). Result: creative-standard follows `WORKFLOW[STATE_INITIALIZED] = prep → STATE_PREPPED → plan` so prep runs.
     - **light** → return `{STATE_PLANNED: [Transition("finalize", STATE_GATED)], STATE_CRITIQUED: [Transition("revise", STATE_GATED)], STATE_EXECUTED: []}`. The new STATE_PLANNED override skips critique entirely (jumping straight to finalize, then to execute via the existing STATE_GATED → finalize → STATE_FINALIZED → execute path); the inherited STATE_CRITIQUED/EXECUTED entries stay so the workflow is internally consistent if anything tries to re-enter. Importantly: **do not** inherit standard's `STATE_INITIALIZED → plan` override, so light still runs prep (1 cut + 1 spark per the brief).
     - **robust** / **superrobust** → return `{}` (no override; full critique cycles).
     - **tiny** → unchanged.
3. **Wire** `workflow_next()` (or whatever currently consults `_ROBUSTNESS_OVERRIDES[robustness]`) to call `_resolve_overrides(robustness, creative=is_creative_mode(state))` instead. Code-mode behavior is unchanged.

   ```python
   if rework_requested and is_creative_mode(state):
       stop_data = _maker_requested_stop(plan_dir)
       if stop_data is not None:
           _record_maker_stop(state, plan_dir, defense=stop_data["defense"])
           return "success", STATE_DONE, None
   ```
4. **Add** helpers in the same file:
   - `_maker_requested_stop(plan_dir: Path) -> dict | None`: reads `plan_dir/finalize.json`; returns `{"defense": "..."}` if any task has `stop_signal.requested == true`, else `None`. Reads `stop_signal` from `finalize.json` because Step 5 + Step 6 propagate it there.
   - `_record_maker_stop(state, plan_dir, defense)`: writes a `meta.notes` entry and updates `directors_notes.json`'s last `passes[]` entry with `stop_requested=true` + `stop_defense=defense`.
5. **Update existing tests** that exercise `_resolve_review_outcome()` (search `tests/` for direct calls) to pass a `plan_dir` argument — typically a `tmp_path` fixture.
6. **Resolves FLAG-002 / issue_hints-2 / correctness-2 / callers / FLAG-004 (data-path consumer half).**

### Step 12: Director's notes assembled at the aggregate boundary only (`megaplan/execute/core.py`, `megaplan/forms/directors_notes.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Add** `megaplan/forms/directors_notes.py`:
   - `update_directors_notes_at_aggregate(plan_dir, state, aggregate_payload, *, iteration, voice, fired_provocations) -> None`: appends a single `passes[]` entry derived from the aggregate payload's `task_updates[].stance` and `stop_signal` fields. Reads/writes `plan_dir/directors_notes.json` atomically. Uses `iteration` as an idempotency key — if a `passes[]` entry already exists with that iteration number, it is *replaced*, not appended. **(Resolves scope.)**
2. **Hook** ONLY into `_build_aggregate_execution_payload()` at `megaplan/execute/core.py` (call sites at lines `431, 679, 1033`): when `is_creative_mode(state)`, call `update_directors_notes_at_aggregate(...)` once per execute run with the final aggregate. **Do NOT hook into `_merge_batch_results()`** — that function only propagates `stance` / `stop_signal` into `finalize.json` (Step 6) and never touches the sidecar.
3. **Hook** into `handle_critique()` so the *initial* triad is recorded in `directors_notes.json` immediately after critique writes its output, even when execution hasn't run yet. Use the same idempotency-key-by-iteration semantics — re-running critique on the same iteration replaces, doesn't append.
4. **Resolves scope-2 (single hook) and scope (idempotency).**

### Step 13: Stance validation enforcement (`megaplan/execute/core.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Update** `_merge_batch_results()` (`megaplan/execute/core.py`) to call `validate_stance(task_update["stance"])` for each task update with a stance, when `is_creative_mode(state)`. Violations are stored on the task as `stance_violations: list[str]` (a new field added to `merge_fields` in Step 6 if needed; otherwise written through to the merged task record directly).
2. **Bubble** violations into the next critique pass: the critique prompt builder (Step 9) reads `directors_notes.json` and, if the prior pass had `stance_violations`, prepends a stance-authenticity sub-provocation: "Read it aloud. If it's safe, the work is too. Rewrite the part that would embarrass you to say to a stranger."
3. **Soft enforcement only** (no hard reject).

---

## Phase 4: Tests + validation

### Step 14: Pure-function unit tests (`tests/test_modes_helpers.py`, `tests/test_forms_registry.py`, `tests/test_provocations.py`, `tests/test_stance_validator.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **`tests/test_modes_helpers.py`**: `is_creative_mode` true for `mode=creative` and `mode=joke`; `creative_form_id` returns `"joke"` for legacy joke state with no form key, returns `state.config.form` for new state, returns `None` for code mode. `is_prose_mode` true for doc/joke/creative.
2. **`tests/test_forms_registry.py`**: `get_form("joke")` and `get_form("poem")` exist; both have ≥3 cuts/forces/sparks; joke has 5 beat IDs, poem has 3; both declare `execution_schema_key == "execution_doc.json"`.
3. **`tests/test_provocations.py`**: light=2, standard=3, robust=3 with rotating voice; `prior_provocation_ids` filters duplicates on standard iteration 2; `over_explained` tag biases selection.
4. **`tests/test_stance_validator.py`**: valid passes; >50 words → violation; hedging verb → violation; third-person → violation; no claim marker → violation.

### Step 15: Workflow + handler integration tests (`tests/test_handle_init_creative_mode.py`, `tests/test_workflow_creative.py`, `tests/test_review_stop_affordance.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **`tests/test_handle_init_creative_mode.py`**: `--mode creative --form joke --output …` succeeds and writes `state.config.form="joke"`; `--mode creative` without `--form` raises `invalid_args`; `--mode creative --form poem --primary-criterion "tightest image"` accepted.
2. **Update** `tests/test_handle_init_joke_mode.py`: `--mode joke` still works AND now writes `state.config.form="joke"`.
3. **`tests/test_workflow_creative.py`** — three explicit assertions:
   - `workflow_next()` for creative-standard at `STATE_INITIALIZED` returns `prep` (not `plan`); code-standard returns `plan` (no regression).
   - `workflow_next()` for creative-light at `STATE_INITIALIZED` returns `prep` (creative-light runs prep, unlike code-light); code-light returns `plan`.
   - `workflow_next()` for creative-light at `STATE_PLANNED` returns `finalize` (skips critique); code-light returns `critique` (no regression). **(Resolves FLAG-005 / issue_hints test coverage.)**
4. **`tests/test_review_stop_affordance.py`**: build a state with a needs_rework review and a `stop_signal.requested=true` task in finalize; assert `_resolve_review_outcome(plan_dir, …)` returns `("success", STATE_DONE, None)` and `meta.notes` records the defense. Without the stop signal, the same input returns `("needs_rework", STATE_FINALIZED, "execute")`. Code mode with stop_signal does NOT short-circuit. Also assert the resolver signature accepts `plan_dir` as the first positional argument. **(Resolves correctness-2 / callers test coverage.)**

### Step 16: Schema + runtime-layout tests (`tests/test_runtime_layout_creative.py`, expand `tests/test_execute_merge_creative.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **`tests/test_runtime_layout_creative.py`**: call `ensure_runtime_layout()`; assert `.megaplan/schemas/execution_doc.json` exists and includes `stance` + `stop_signal` properties; assert `.megaplan/schemas/finalize.json` exists and includes `stance` + `stop_signal` on `tasks[]`; assert `.megaplan/schemas/directors_notes.json` exists; assert `get_execution_schema_key("creative", "poem") == "execution_doc.json"`.
2. **`tests/test_execute_merge_creative.py`**: build a synthetic execution_doc payload with a `task_update` containing `stance` + `stop_signal`; call `_merge_batch_results(..., mode="creative")`; assert both fields land on the corresponding task in `finalize_data["tasks"]`. **(Resolves FLAG-004 / correctness-1 test coverage.)**

### Step 17: Prompt builder tests (`tests/test_prompts_creative.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **`tests/test_prompts_creative.py`**:
   - `_critique_creative_prompt(form=joke, robustness="standard")` mentions exactly 1 cut, 1 force, 1 spark by name.
   - `_critique_creative_prompt(form=poem, ...)` references poem beats, not screenplay beats.
   - `_execute_creative_prompt` includes stance + stop-affordance instructions.
   - Code-mode `_execute_prompt` does NOT include stop-affordance instructions.
   - Iteration 2 critique excludes provocation IDs from a fixture `directors_notes.json`.
   - Dispatcher: `create_claude_prompt("critique", state_with_mode_joke, …)` ≡ `create_claude_prompt("critique", state_with_mode_creative_form_joke, …)`.
   - `select_active_checks(state, robustness)` returns the same output as `joke_checks_for_robustness(robustness)` for joke (back-compat).

### Step 18: End-to-end smoke + idempotency (`tests/test_creative_mode_smoke.py`, `tests/test_directors_notes_idempotency.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **`tests/test_creative_mode_smoke.py`**:
   - `--mode creative --form poem --output poems/p.md` → prep+plan+critique+revise+execute+review.
   - Mocked execute returns a valid stance + `stop_signal.requested=false`. Assert `directors_notes.json` is written exactly once per execute run, contains form, primary_criterion, fired provocations, stances.
   - Second run: mocked execute returns `stop_signal.requested=true`, mocked review returns `needs_rework`. Assert review halts with STATE_DONE and `meta.notes` records the defense.
2. **`tests/test_directors_notes_idempotency.py`**: simulate a multi-batch execute; assert `directors_notes.json` has exactly ONE `passes[]` entry per iteration (not one per batch). Re-run aggregate assembly with the same iteration; assert the entry is replaced, not duplicated. **(Resolves scope test coverage.)**

### Step 19: Run focused tests, then full suite
**Scope:** Small

1. **Run** unit tests (Steps 14, 16) — fastest, no fixtures.
2. **Run** init/workflow/review tests (Step 15).
3. **Run** prompt builder tests (Step 17).
4. **Run** smoke + idempotency (Step 18) and existing `tests/test_joke_mode_smoke.py`.
5. **Run** full `pytest tests/` to catch regressions, especially anywhere a `mode in {"doc", "joke"}` site was rewritten or `_resolve_review_outcome()` is called.

---

## Execution Order

1. **Phase 1** (helpers + registry + schemas + workflow) lands first.
2. Inside Phase 1: Step 1 (helpers) → Step 2 (forms registry) → Step 3 (init/CLI) → Step 4 (workflow override) → Step 5 (schemas + runtime layout).
3. **Phase 2** (mode-aware sweep + merge_fields extension) is mechanical and runs after Phase 1.
4. **Phase 3** (provocation engine + stance + stop-affordance + sidecar) depends on Steps 1, 2, 6 being done.
5. **Phase 4 tests** can begin in parallel with Phase 3 once Steps 1-8 are committed.
6. The riskiest moment is Step 6 (mode sweep + merge_fields); land it green before starting Phase 3. Step 11 (resolver signature change) should also land before Step 12 so smoke tests can exercise the full halt path.

## Validation Order

1. Pure-function unit tests (helpers, registry, selection, stance validator).
2. Schema + runtime-layout tests + merge-fields propagation test.
3. Workflow + handler tests (including the three new workflow_next assertions and the resolver signature assertion).
4. Prompt builder tests.
5. End-to-end smoke + idempotency tests with mocked agents.
6. Full pytest suite.

  "meta_commentary": "Phased execution: T1\u2192T2 are pure-foundation. T3\u2192T5 land the contracts (CLI, workflow, schema). T6 is the riskiest single step (mechanical sweep across ~25 sites + critical merge_fields extension); land it green before starting Phase 3. T11 (resolver signature) should land before T12 so smoke tests exercise the full halt path. T13 depends on T6+T10+T12 \u2014 leave it last in Phase 3.\n\nCritical execution-time decision at T4: the v3 plan proposes STATE_PLANNED\u2192finalize\u2192STATE_GATED for creative-light, but handle_finalize() requires STATE_GATED as the *current* state. Per the user note, prefer the cheap fix (loosen require_state in megaplan/handlers/finalize.py:211-213 with `if not (is_creative_mode(state) and configured_robustness(state)=='light'): require_state(state, \"finalize\", {STATE_GATED})`). If that propagates to handle_execute() rejecting the same state, fall back to building a creative-light auto-pass gate handler (option a). Test in T15 will reveal which is needed.\n\nThe forms registry (T2) is the leverage point that prevents the *_creative.py prompt files from ballooning. All form-specific text \u2014 beat names, voice personas, full provocation prompt text \u2014 lives in registry data classes, not in prompt builders. This is a hard success criterion ('No new prompt file is forked per form'). If T9's prompt files start exceeding ~250 lines, that's a signal to push more into T2.\n\nThe stop-signal data path (FLAG-004) has three load-bearing pieces that must all land together: (1) T5 adds stop_signal to finalize.json schema, (2) T6 extends prose-mode merge_fields to include stop_signal, (3) T11's _maker_requested_stop reads it from finalize.json. Missing any one makes the whole chain a silent no-op. T16's test_execute_merge_creative is the regression guard.\n\nSidecar idempotency (scope flag): committed to ONE hook in _build_aggregate_execution_payload (plus initial-triad hook in handle_critique). Iteration is the idempotency key \u2014 re-running aggregate replaces, doesn't append. Don't accidentally hook from _merge_batch_results too; that's the failure mode the scope flag was raised about.\n\nThe plan flagged 16 unresolved 'significant' flags but the orchestrator force-proceeded. Reading them carefully: 12 of the 16 are duplicates/aliases of the same creative-light handle_finalize cluster (FLAG-005, issue_hints, scope, callers, correctness, all_locations all describe the same root cause). The user note explicitly resolves it. Don't get spooked by the flag count \u2014 it's one fault, not sixteen.\n\nValidation order matches Phase 4: pure unit tests first (cheapest, no fixtures), then integration, then smoke, then full suite + grep checks. T19 finishes with a throwaway repro script \u2014 this is required by the briefing template ('Additionally, the executor should write a short throwaway script that reproduces the specific bug described in the task, run it to confirm the fix works, then delete the script'). For this work the 'bug' is that creative+poem doesn't exist yet, so the repro confirms the new path is wired end-to-end at the CLI level.\n\nTwo open design branches the prep phase deferred (per plan_v3 questions): (1) Step 4's creative-light bridge \u2014 the plan picked finalize-as-bridge over a separate light-execute path (correct per assumption #3); (2) Step 11's resolver signature \u2014 picked positional plan_dir over precomputed-data kwarg (per assumption #2). Both are settled; no need to revisit.",

Confirm finalize-as-bridge is right for light, vs. having a separate light-execute path that reads the plan directly.",
    "Step 11 adds `plan_dir` as the first positional parameter to `_resolve_review_outcome()`. Alternative: accept `stop_signal_data: dict | None` precomputed by the caller, keeping the resolver pure. The positional `plan_dir` is simpler but couples the resolver to disk; the precomputed-data form is cleaner long-term. Either is fine \u2014 confirm preference."
  ],

  "assumptions": [
    "Adding `stop_signal` to `merge_fields` and to `finalize.json` `tasks[]` is the right place for the stop-signal source of truth. Alternative (read from execution.json or directors_notes.json) would be more decoupled but require duplicating the propagation logic. Finalize is already the merge-target authority.",
    "`_resolve_review_outcome()` takes `plan_dir` as the first positional parameter (not a kwarg) because both verified call sites can pass it positionally without ambiguity, and adding kwargs to a function with this many positional args invites future bugs. Existing tests in `tests/` that mock or directly call this resolver will need a `tmp_path` fixture pass-through.",
    "For creative-light at STATE_PLANNED, the chosen override is `Transition('finalize', STATE_GATED)` so the workflow goes plan \u2192 finalize \u2192 STATE_FINALIZED \u2192 execute \u2192 STATE_EXECUTED \u2192 (light-overridden empty) \u2192 done. This skips critique+gate but keeps finalize so executable tasks are still produced from the canvas.",
    "Director's notes uses `iteration` (not `(iteration, batch_index)`) as the idempotency key because Step 12 hooks only at the aggregate boundary, not per-batch. One pass entry per iteration matches the brief's 'pass' semantics.",
    "The v2 plan's claim that prep stays in STATE_INITIALIZED was wrong; verified `WORKFLOW[STATE_INITIALIZED] = [Transition('prep', STATE_PREPPED)]`. Step 4 is now correct.",
    "All settled decisions from iter 1-2 are preserved verbatim: helpers trio as single source of truth, joke alias preserved, structured stance, JSON-only sidecar, fixed roster of provocateur voices, formal robustness table, primary criterion lifted to all creative modes, poem as second form, soft stance enforcement, form-declared execution_schema_key, select_active_checks dispatcher, directors-notes at merge boundary."
  ],
  "delta_from_previous_percent": 51.45,
  "structure_warnings": []

      "concern": "Did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: Creative workflow mapping: the brief says the prep phase chooses targeted provocations and that standard creative mode gets a full triad on prep, but the current workflow overrides standard robustness from initialized directly to plan in `megaplan/_core/workflow.py:78-82`.       "category": "completeness",

      "evidence": "Restructured into 4 phases: (1) added a single-source-of-truth `is_creative_mode(state)` / `creative_form_id(state)` / `is_prose_mode(state)` helper trio so every mode-aware site has one place to consult; (2) called out a mechanical sweep step naming all ~25 verified `mode in {\"doc\", \"joke\"}` and `mode == \"joke\"` sites by file:line and replacing each with the helper; (3) made the workflow override mode-aware so creative-standard runs prep (FLAG-001); (4) moved the stop-affordance check from the dead approved-review branch to the live needs_rework branch in `_resolve_review_outcome()` (FLAG-002); (5) integrated director's notes at the merge boundary inside `_merge_batch_results()` instead of crawling artifacts (scope-2); (6) declared per-form `execution_schema_key` so callers pass form, not just mode (callers-1, FLAG-003); (7) routed Hermes template rewriter, mock workers, and `handle_critique()` through a shared `select_active_checks(state, robustness)` dispatcher (correctness-2, callers-2); (8) added a runtime-layout test asserting the new schema entries reach `.megaplan/schemas/` (all_locations-1); (9) stamped `form` onto state at init time and added `creative_form_id()` so back-compat with legacy joke state files is contract-shaped, not ad-hoc (all_locations-2).       "concern": "Did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: Stop-affordance: the brief's reason for the maker stop signal is to prevent additional passes from damaging the piece, but the plan says to halt further critique/revise cycles only when `stop_signal.requested=true` and the review verdict is approved.       "concern": "Are the proposed changes technically correct?: Stop-signal persistence is internally inconsistent. In the current merge code, `_validate_and_merge_batch()` only copies fields listed in `merge_fields` (`megaplan/execute/core.py:307-315`), so a sibling `task_update.stop_signal` would not be present in `finalize.json` unless the plan explicitly adds it to the finalize schema and merge fields, or reads it from `execution.json`/`directors_notes.json` instead.",
      "category": "correctness",
      "severity_hint": "likely-significant",
      "evidence": "Tightly scoped revision addressing only the four named clusters from the gate. (A) Stop-signal data path: Step 5 now adds `stop_signal` to `finalize.json` `tasks[]` schema (not just `stance`); Step 6 extends `_merge_batch_results()`'s prose-mode `merge_fields` tuple to include both `stance` and `stop_signal` so they propagate from execution_doc \u2192 finalize. New test in Step 16 (`test_execute_merge_creative.py`) verifies propagation. (B) Resolver signature: Step 11 adds `plan_dir: Path` as the first positional parameter to `_resolve_review_outcome()`, updates both verified call sites at `megaplan/handlers/review.py:332-337` and `483-488`, and explicitly notes existing tests for that resolver need their fixtures updated. New success criterion gates this. (C) Light workflow override: Step 4 now distinguishes `creative-standard` (drops the standard skip-prep override so prep runs) from `creative-light` (adds a `STATE_PLANNED \u2192 finalize` override so critique is skipped, while NOT inheriting standard's skip-prep override so creative-light still runs prep). Also caught and fixed a v2 bug: prep transitions to STATE_PREPPED, not STATE_INITIALIZED. Step 15 adds three explicit `workflow_next()` assertions covering creative-standard at INITIALIZED, creative-light at INITIALIZED, and creative-light at PLANNED, plus code-mode regression checks. (D) Sidecar idempotency: Step 12 commits to a single hook (`_build_aggregate_execution_payload()` only, never `_merge_batch_results()`) and adds an iteration-keyed replace-not-append semantic; new `test_directors_notes_idempotency.py` in Step 18 verifies no duplicate `passes[]` entries per iteration. No other steps were re-litigated; all settled decisions from iter 1-2 are preserved verbatim.",

    "iteration": 3,
    "idea": "Implement a creative-work mode in megaplan, generalizing today's `--mode joke` into a `--mode creative --form <form>` system with a real provocation engine.\n\n## The provocation engine\n\nThe brief fires a small set of provocations at the executor \u2014 adversarial prompts that demand an act on the work, not introspection. Three vectors:\n\n- **Cuts** \u2014 strip / expose / kill. Example: \"Cut the line you'd fight hardest to keep. If the piece is worse, you were right; if it's the same, you killed a darling.\"\n- **Forces** \u2014 perform a transformation on the piece. Example: \"Halve it. Write the line that survives.\"\n- **Sparks** \u2014 the brief itself supplies foreign material the maker reacts to. This is the new vector vs joke mode today. Sub-types:\n  - Hostile gifts (an image, line, or detail invented in the brief and asserted to belong)\n  - Substitutions (\"what if the protagonist were the antagonist of this same scene\")\n  - Inversions (\"the version of this that's a confession, not a performance\")\n  - Forced transplants (steal a structure from a different form: tarot spread, recipe, courtroom transcript)\n  - Borrowed moves (steal one specific move from a master in this form, named)\n  - Hostile claims (\"this piece is afraid of the thing it's actually about\" \u2014 refute or absorb)\n  - Generative constraints (half the words forbidden, no character speaks, must rhyme)\n  - Sensory escalation (what does it smell like / sound like in the next room)\n  - Dares (name the move that would get you booed; try it)\n\nDefault firing pattern: **one cut, one force, one spark per pass.** Three is plenty; five is a lecture. The prep phase chooses the specific provocations targeted at the draft state \u2014 over-explained drafts get cuts on the unsaid; safe drafts get risk/dare sparks; bloated drafts get force-halve; replaceable-feeling drafts get borrowed-move + \"who could have made this.\"\n\n## The stance\n\nEvery executor task output includes a stance field: in the maker's authentic voice, naming the provocation engaged, the angle taken, and what changed. Hard constraints to prevent LLM-flavor sand:\n\n- \u226450 words\n- First person\n- Names the specific provocation engaged\n- No hedging verbs (considered / tried / attempted / sought to)\n- Must take a position someone could disagree with\n\nA sub-provocation fires at the stance itself: \"Read it aloud. If it's safe, the work is too. Rewrite the part that would embarrass you to say to a stranger.\"\n\nStance lives in per-task receipts and is bubbled into a \"director's notes\" sidecar artifact. NOT inline in the final piece \u2014 the artifact must stand alone.\n\n## Robustness mapping\n\nCreative work has a different process curve than code: it peaks and then declines into over-sanded mush. Robustness should mean *different angles, harder targets, more diverse provocateur voices* \u2014 not just more cycles.\n\n- **Light** \u2014 single pass, one cut + one spark (skip the force), stance \u22642 sentences, no critique cycle. For sketches and throwaways. Failure mode protected against: over-process killing a quick instinct.\n- **Standard** \u2014 one critique/revise cycle. Full triad on prep. Critique fires *different* provocations from a different angle (no relitigating addressed material). Revise stance must explicitly contrast the prior stance.\n- **Robust** \u2014 multiple cycles with *different provocateur voices*: formalist (structure/economy), iconoclast (risk/lie/dare), audience (stakes/specificity for one named person). Review evaluates full scene canvas + stance authenticity + alignment.\n\n## Executor stop-affordance\n\nLet the maker signal \"the next pass would damage the piece\" with a stance defending why. Code mode shouldn't have this (more review = safer); creative must. Without it, robust mode reliably over-edits.\n\n## Generalize joke \u2192 creative-work\n\n- New mode: `--mode creative --form <form>` with joke as the first registered form\n- Existing `--mode joke` continues to work as an alias for `--mode creative --form joke` \u2014 no breakage of existing plans\n- Prove the abstraction by adding a second form: prep phase picks the cheapest validation form (poem or short ad copy)\n- Form-specific prompt fragments inject into shared scaffolds; we do NOT fork five new prompt files per form\n\n## Design branches the prep phase should resolve\n\nThe following are real branches with arguments either way \u2014 settle them in prep, not in code:\n\n1. Stance field shape: single string vs structured (`challenge_engaged` / `angle_taken` / `what_changed`)\n2. Director's notes destination: separate sidecar file, YAML frontmatter on the artifact, or only in receipts\n3. Stop-affordance signal: new outcome status, stance flag, or separate halt-task type\n4. Provocateur voices in robust mode: fixed roster (formalist / iconoclast / audience) vs model-chosen per cycle\n5. Robustness \u2192 provocation-count mapping: formal table vs executor discretion within bounds\n6. Whether to retire `--primary-criterion` as joke-only and lift it to creative-work-wide, or keep both surfaces\n\n## Out of scope\n\n- New forms beyond the second validation form\n- Non-text creative outputs (audio/visual)\n- Team-of-makers workflows\n- Backwards-compat for any pre-v0.21 joke-mode plan files (current plans untouched; new plans use new schema)\n\n## Primary criterion (for prep guidance)\n\nSharpest provocation system that survives over-process. The system should produce work that has *a stance*, took risks, made specific choices, and refused something \u2014 and should produce that work without grinding it into nervous mush via too many cycles. If the system is \"more rigorous\" but the output is more sanded, we have failed. The stance authenticity check is the single most load-bearing piece \u2014 it is what converts the system from \"external pressure\" into \"training the maker as their own provocateur.\"",

        "resolution": "Tightly scoped revision addressing only the four named clusters from the gate. (A) Stop-signal data path: Step 5 now adds `stop_signal` to `finalize.json` `tasks[]` schema (not just `stance`); Step 6 extends `_merge_batch_results()`'s prose-mode `merge_fields` tuple to include both `stance` and `stop_signal` so they propagate from execution_doc \u2192 finalize. New test in Step 16 (`test_execute_merge_creative.py`) verifies propagation. (B) Resolver signature: Step 11 adds `plan_dir: Path` as the first positional parameter to `_resolve_review_outcome()`, updates both verified call sites at `megaplan/handlers/review.py:332-337` and `483-488`, and explicitly notes existing tests for that resolver need their fixtures updated. New success criterion gates this. (C) Light workflow override: Step 4 now distinguishes `creative-standard` (drops the standard skip-prep override so prep runs) from `creative-light` (adds a `STATE_PLANNED \u2192 finalize` override so critique is skipped, while NOT inheriting standard's skip-prep override so creative-light still runs prep). Also caught and fixed a v2 bug: prep transitions to STATE_PREPPED, not STATE_INITIALIZED. Step 15 adds three explicit `workflow_next()` assertions covering creative-standard at INITIALIZED, creative-light at INITIALIZED, and creative-light at PLANNED, plus code-mode regression checks. (D) Sidecar idempotency: Step 12 commits to a single hook (`_build_aggregate_execution_payload()` only, never `_merge_batch_results()`) and adds an iteration-keyed replace-not-append semantic; new `test_directors_notes_idempotency.py` in Step 18 verifies no duplicate `passes[]` entries per iteration. No other steps were re-litigated; all settled decisions from iter 1-2 are preserved verbatim."

In the current merge code, `_validate_and_merge_batch()` only copies fields listed in `merge_fields` (`megaplan/execute/core.py:307-315`), so a sibling `task_update.stop_signal` would not be present in `finalize.json` unless the plan explicitly adds it to the finalize schema and merge fields, or reads it from `execution.json`/`directors_notes.json` instead.",
    "severity": "significant",
    "status": "addressed"
  },
  {
    "id": "correctness-2",
    "concern": "Are the proposed changes technically correct?: Step 11's pseudocode calls `_maker_requested_stop(plan_dir)` from inside `_resolve_review_outcome()`, but the current `_resolve_review_outcome()` signature has no `plan_dir` parameter and both call sites pass only review counts, robustness, state, issues, and criteria (`megaplan/handlers/review.py:148-190`, `332-337`, `483-488`). The plan needs to specify adding `plan_dir` or pass precomputed stop-signal data into the resolver; otherwise the helper has no path to read finalize or directors-notes data.",
    "severity": "significant",
    "status": "addressed"
  },
  {
    "id": "scope-1",
    "concern": "Search for related code that handles the same concept. Is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: Mode aliasing is broader than init and prompt builders: `rg` found mode-specific surfaces in CLI status display (`megaplan/cli.py:304-307`), execute timeout auditing (`megaplan/execute/timeout.py:77-84`), review pre-check gating (`megaplan/handlers/review.py:283-286`), schema selection, execute merge, workers, and Hermes. The plan's file list mentions some of these categories generically, but it does not call out that every prose-artifact check currently written as `doc|joke` needs a creative/form-aware helper.",
    "severity": "significant",
    "status": "addressed"
  },
  {
    "id": "scope-2",
    "concern": "Search for related code that handles the same concept. Is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: The plan's proposed directors-notes assembler walks plan-dir artifacts for per-iteration critique flags, finalize stances, and execute stop signals, but current execution merge already normalizes task updates into `finalize.json` in `_merge_batch_results()` and aggregate payloads in `_build_aggregate_execution_payload()`. This broader data-flow suggests the sidecar writer should be integrated at merge/review boundaries rather than implemented as a separate artifact crawler that can drift from the source-of-truth merge logic.",
    "severity": "significant",
    "status": "addressed"
  },
  {
    "id": "all_locations-1",
    "concern": "Does the change touch all locations AND supporting infrastructure?: Generated/runtime schema infrastructure is a required location. `ensure_runtime_layout()` writes every schema from `SCHEMAS` to `.megaplan/schemas` (`megaplan/_core/io.py:306-312`), and worker invocations read those disk schemas via `schemas_root()` (`megaplan/workers.py:1289-1290`, `1395-1396`; `megaplan/hermes_worker.py:281-283`). The plan updates `SCHEMAS` but does not include a runtime-schema assertion for `execution_doc.json`/creative mode or any new sidecar schema, so schema additions may not be tested through the same path agents actually consume.",
    "severity": "significant",
    "status": "addressed"
  },
  {
    "id": "all_locations-2",
    "concern": "Does the change touch all locations AND supporting infrastructure?: The plan says to keep `--mode joke` working as an alias while new plans set `mode='creative'` and `form='joke'`, but it does not include a migration/helper contract for existing state files where `mode='joke'` and no `form` exists outside prompt dispatch. Current callers read `state['config'].get('mode')` directly across execute, review, workers, and CLI status; without a shared `is_creative_mode()`/`creative_form_id()` helper, it is easy for back-compat to work in prompts but fail in execution or validation.",
    "severity": "significant",
    "status": "addressed"
  },
  {
    "id": "callers-1",
    "concern": "Find the callers of the changed function. What arguments do they actually pass?     "severity": "significant",
    "status": "addressed"
  },
  {

    "concern": "Find the callers of the changed function. What arguments do they actually pass?     "status": "addressed"

Without that glue, the light path cannot reach execute.",
    "severity": "significant",

    "status": "open"
  },

        Requirements:
        - Verify each success criterion explicitly.
        - Trust executor evidence by default. Dig deeper only where the git diff, `execution_audit.json`, or vague notes make the claim ambiguous.
        - Each criterion has a `priority` (`must`, `should`, or `info`). Apply these rules:
          - `must` criteria are hard gates. A `must` criterion that fails means `needs_rework`.
          - `should` criteria are quality targets. If the spirit is met but the letter is not, mark `pass` with evidence explaining the gap. Only mark `fail` if the intent was clearly missed. A `should` failure alone does NOT require `needs_rework`.
          - `info` criteria are for human reference. Mark them `waived` with a note — do not evaluate them.
          - If a criterion has `requires` capabilities that are not satisfiable by container workers (e.g., `drive_browser`, `subjective_judgment`), mark it `deferred_human` — NOT `fail` or `waived`. Deferred-human criteria do NOT count toward `needs_rework`.
          - If a criterion (any priority) cannot be verified in this context (e.g., requires manual testing or runtime observation), mark it `waived` with an explanation.
        - Set `review_verdict` to `needs_rework` only when at least one `must` criterion fails or actual implementation work is incomplete. Use `approved` when all `must` criteria pass, even if some `should` criteria are flagged.

        - baseline_test_failures in finalize.json lists tests that were already failing before execution. Do not flag these as rework items unless the executor introduced new failures in those same tests.
        - Cross-reference each task's `files_changed` and `commands_run` against the git diff and any audit findings.
        - Review every `sense_check` explicitly and treat perfunctory acknowledgments as a reason to dig deeper.
        - Follow this JSON shape exactly:
        ```json
        {
          "review_verdict": "approved",
          "criteria": [
            {
              "name": "All existing tests pass",
              "priority": "must",
              "pass": "pass",
              "evidence": "Test suite ran green — 42 passed, 0 failed."
            },
            {
              "name": "File under ~300 lines",
              "priority": "should",
              "pass": "pass",
              "evidence": "File is 375 lines — above the target but reasonable given the component's responsibilities. Spirit met."
            },
            {
              "name": "Manual smoke tests pass",
              "priority": "info",
              "pass": "waived",
              "evidence": "Cannot be verified in automated review. Noted for manual QA."
            }
          ],
          "issues": [],
          "rework_items": [],
          "summary": "Approved. All must criteria pass. The should criterion on line count is close enough given the component scope.",
          "task_verdicts": [
            {
              "task_id": "T6",
              "reviewer_verdict": "Pass. Claimed handler changes and command evidence match the repo state.",
              "evidence_files": ["megaplan/handlers.py", "megaplan/evaluation.py"]
            }
          ],
          "sense_check_verdicts": [
            {
              "sense_check_id": "SC6",
              "verdict": "Confirmed. The execute blocker only fires when both evidence arrays are empty."
            }
          ]
        }
        ```
        - `rework_items` must be an array of structured rework directives. When `review_verdict` is `needs_rework`, populate one entry per issue with:
          - `task_id`: which finalize task this issue relates to
          - `issue`: what is wrong
          - `expected`: what correct behavior looks like
          - `actual`: what was observed
          - `evidence_file` (optional): file path supporting the finding
          - `flag_id`: critique/review flag ID when applicable, otherwise `null`
          - `source`: short machine-readable source tag when applicable, otherwise `null`
        - `issues` must still be populated as a flat one-line-per-item summary derived from `rework_items` (for backward compatibility). When approved, both `issues` and `rework_items` should be empty arrays.
        - When the work needs another execute pass, keep the same shape and change only `review_verdict` to `needs_rework`; make `issues`, `rework_items`, `summary`, and task verdicts specific enough for the executor to act on directly.

You are already running inside the megaplan harness for this step. Do the requested planning/review/execution work directly.         /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan

        Idea:
Implement a creative-work mode in megaplan, generalizing today's `--mode joke` into a `--mode creative --form <form>` system with a real provocation engine.

## The provocation engine

The brief fires a small set of provocations at the executor — adversarial prompts that demand an act on the work, not introspection. Three vectors:

- **Cuts** — strip / expose / kill. Example: "Cut the line you'd fight hardest to keep. If the piece is worse, you were right; if it's the same, you killed a darling."
- **Forces** — perform a transformation on the piece. Example: "Halve it. Write the line that survives."
- **Sparks** — the brief itself supplies foreign material the maker reacts to. This is the new vector vs joke mode today. Sub-types:
  - Hostile gifts (an image, line, or detail invented in the brief and asserted to belong)
  - Substitutions ("what if the protagonist were the antagonist of this same scene")
  - Inversions ("the version of this that's a confession, not a performance")
  - Forced transplants (steal a structure from a different form: tarot spread, recipe, courtroom transcript)
  - Borrowed moves (steal one specific move from a master in this form, named)
  - Hostile claims ("this piece is afraid of the thing it's actually about" — refute or absorb)
  - Generative constraints (half the words forbidden, no character speaks, must rhyme)
  - Sensory escalation (what does it smell like / sound like in the next room)
  - Dares (name the move that would get you booed; try it)

Default firing pattern: **one cut, one force, one spark per pass.** Three is plenty; five is a lecture. The prep phase chooses the specific provocations targeted at the draft state — over-explained drafts get cuts on the unsaid; safe drafts get risk/dare sparks; bloated drafts get force-halve; replaceable-feeling drafts get borrowed-move + "who could have made this."

## The stance

Every executor task output includes a stance field: in the maker's authentic voice, naming the provocation engaged, the angle taken, and what changed. Hard constraints to prevent LLM-flavor sand:

- ≤50 words
- First person
- Names the specific provocation engaged
- No hedging verbs (considered / tried / attempted / sought to)
- Must take a position someone could disagree with

A sub-provocation fires at the stance itself: "Read it aloud. If it's safe, the work is too. Rewrite the part that would embarrass you to say to a stranger."

Stance lives in per-task receipts and is bubbled into a "director's notes" sidecar artifact. NOT inline in the final piece — the artifact must stand alone.

## Robustness mapping

Creative work has a different process curve than code: it peaks and then declines into over-sanded mush. Robustness should mean *different angles, harder targets, more diverse provocateur voices* — not just more cycles.

- **Light** — single pass, one cut + one spark (skip the force), stance ≤2 sentences, no critique cycle. For sketches and throwaways. Failure mode protected against: over-process killing a quick instinct.
- **Standard** — one critique/revise cycle. Full triad on prep. Critique fires *different* provocations from a different angle (no relitigating addressed material). Revise stance must explicitly contrast the prior stance.
- **Robust** — multiple cycles with *different provocateur voices*: formalist (structure/economy), iconoclast (risk/lie/dare), audience (stakes/specificity for one named person). Review evaluates full scene canvas + stance authenticity + alignment.

## Executor stop-affordance

Let the maker signal "the next pass would damage the piece" with a stance defending why. Code mode shouldn't have this (more review = safer); creative must. Without it, robust mode reliably over-edits.

## Generalize joke → creative-work

- New mode: `--mode creative --form <form>` with joke as the first registered form
- Existing `--mode joke` continues to work as an alias for `--mode creative --form joke` — no breakage of existing plans
- Prove the abstraction by adding a second form: prep phase picks the cheapest validation form (poem or short ad copy)
- Form-specific prompt fragments inject into shared scaffolds; we do NOT fork five new prompt files per form

## Design branches the prep phase should resolve

The following are real branches with arguments either way — settle them in prep, not in code:

1. Stance field shape: single string vs structured (`challenge_engaged` / `angle_taken` / `what_changed`)
2. Director's notes destination: separate sidecar file, YAML frontmatter on the artifact, or only in receipts
3. Stop-affordance signal: new outcome status, stance flag, or separate halt-task type
4. Provocateur voices in robust mode: fixed roster (formalist / iconoclast / audience) vs model-chosen per cycle
5. Robustness → provocation-count mapping: formal table vs executor discretion within bounds
6. Whether to retire `--primary-criterion` as joke-only and lift it to creative-work-wide, or keep both surfaces

## Out of scope

- New forms beyond the second validation form
- Non-text creative outputs (audio/visual)
- Team-of-makers workflows
- Backwards-compat for any pre-v0.21 joke-mode plan files (current plans untouched; new plans use new schema)

## Primary criterion (for prep guidance)

Sharpest provocation system that survives over-process. The system should produce work that has *a stance*, took risks, made specific choices, and refused something — and should produce that work without grinding it into nervous mush via too many cycles. If the system is "more rigorous" but the output is more sanded, we have failed. The stance authenticity check is the single most load-bearing piece — it is what converts the system from "external pressure" into "training the maker as their own provocateur."

User notes and answers:
- Creative-light workflow flag cluster (FLAG-005, issue_hints, scope, callers, correctness, all_locations) all surface the same single issue: handle_finalize() requires STATE_GATED but the v3 plan proposes surfacing finalize from STATE_PLANNED for creative-light. Resolution at implementation time: route creative-light through gate (plan->gate->finalize) like other modes, with a creative-light-specific gate that auto-passes (no critique cycle), rather than skipping gate entirely. This preserves the state machine contract and the brief's intent (single-pass, no critique cycle for creative-light). Executor should pick the cheapest of: (a) creative-light gate auto-pass, or (b) loosening handle_finalize to accept STATE_PLANNED when mode=creative+robustness=light. Recurring critique = same fault, not new fault.

        Approved plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Creative-Work Mode + Provocation Engine

## Overview

Generalize today's `--mode joke` into `--mode creative --form <form>` with a registered-form system, replace the static joke "lens" critique with a real **provocation engine** (cuts / forces / sparks), add a per-task **stance** field with hard constraints, route stances into a **director's notes sidecar** assembled at the merge boundary, add an executor **stop-affordance** at the correct vertex (the rework branch), and remap robustness so higher levels mean *different angles and provocateur voices*, not more cycles.

**Root-cause framing.** The previous draft fixed the prompt-builder layer but every other mode-aware surface (`mode in {"doc", "joke"}` and `mode == "joke"`) is duplicated across the codebase. Verified instances (from grep):
- `megaplan/evaluation.py:124`, `megaplan/handlers/init.py:64`, `megaplan/handlers/execute.py:106`, `megaplan/handlers/critique.py:120`, `megaplan/cli.py:307`, `megaplan/handlers/review.py:273`
- `megaplan/execute/core.py:163, 169, 209, 308, 401, 468, 1085`
- `megaplan/execute/timeout.py:49, 70, 138, 299`
- `megaplan/schemas.py:653` (`get_execution_schema_key`)
- `megaplan/workers.py:819`, `megaplan/hermes_worker.py:439`
- `megaplan/prompts/__init__.py:128, 137`, `megaplan/prompts/critique.py:302`, `megaplan/prompts/finalize.py:32`, `megaplan/prompts/planning.py:103`, `megaplan/prompts/review.py:85`

These *all* answer one of two questions: "is this a prose-artifact mode?" or "which form's prompt builder do I use?" The fix is two helpers — `is_creative_mode(state) -> bool` and `creative_form_id(state) -> str | None` — added once, called everywhere. Same shape as the existing `configured_robustness(state)` helper. This is a mechanical sweep, not new logic.

**Workflow override (FLAG-001 / issue_hints-1 / FLAG-005 / issue_hints).** Verified `WORKFLOW` at `megaplan/_core/workflow.py:34-80`: the natural creative path is `STATE_INITIALIZED → prep → STATE_PREPPED → plan → STATE_PLANNED → critique → STATE_CRITIQUED → ... → execute → STATE_EXECUTED → review → STATE_DONE`. `_ROBUSTNESS_OVERRIDES["standard"]` injects `STATE_INITIALIZED → plan` (skipping prep). `_ROBUSTNESS_OVERRIDES["light"]` adds `STATE_INITIALIZED → plan` (skip prep), `STATE_CRITIQUED → revise → STATE_GATED` (skip gate), `STATE_EXECUTED: []` (skip review) — but does NOT override `STATE_PLANNED`, so light *still runs critique*. For creative we need both: standard runs prep AND light skips critique entirely.

**Stop-affordance at the correct vertex (FLAG-002 / issue_hints-2 / correctness-2 / callers).** `_resolve_review_outcome()` at `megaplan/handlers/review.py:149-201` is the right place to halt a `needs_rework` cycle, but its current signature has no `plan_dir`. Verified call sites at `review.py:332-337` and `483-488` pass only `(review_verdict, verdict_count, total_tasks, check_count, total_checks, missing_evidence, robustness, state, issues, criteria=…)`. Step 11 adds `plan_dir: Path` to the signature and updates both call sites.

**Stop-signal data path (correctness-1 / FLAG-004).** Verified `_merge_batch_results()` at `megaplan/execute/core.py:282-330`: for prose modes the merge field tuple is `("status", "executor_notes", "sections_written")`. Without explicit propagation, `stop_signal` and `stance` from `execution_doc.json` task_updates never reach `finalize.json`. Step 5 adds both to the `finalize.json` schema; Step 6 extends the prose-mode `merge_fields` tuple.

**Director's notes idempotency (scope).** Verified `_build_aggregate_execution_payload()` is called at the end of an execute run from `megaplan/execute/core.py:431, 679, 1033`. To avoid double-appending pass entries (once per batch + once at aggregate), Step 12 commits to a single hook: write `directors_notes.json` ONLY from `_build_aggregate_execution_payload()`, never from `_merge_batch_results()`. The merge function still propagates `stance` / `stop_signal` into `finalize.json`; the sidecar reads from the aggregate.

**Joke alias as a thin shim.** `--mode joke` continues to work. At init time it's stored as `mode = "joke"` (preserved verbatim for in-flight plans) AND `form = "joke"` is stamped onto config. The two helpers (`is_creative_mode`, `creative_form_id`) treat `mode in {"creative", "joke"}` as creative and resolve form from `state.config.form` (or `"joke"` when missing).

The whole change is contained inside `megaplan/cli.py`, `megaplan/handlers/`, `megaplan/prompts/`, `megaplan/audits/robustness.py`, `megaplan/schemas.py`, `megaplan/_core/`, `megaplan/execute/core.py`, `megaplan/execute/timeout.py`, `megaplan/workers.py`, `megaplan/hermes_worker.py`, `megaplan/evaluation.py`, plus a new `megaplan/forms/` package and tests under `tests/`.

## Phase 1: Foundation — mode helpers, forms registry, schemas, workflow override

### Step 1: Add mode helpers (`megaplan/_core/modes.py`, re-export from `megaplan/_core/__init__.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Add** `megaplan/_core/modes.py`:
   ```python
   def is_creative_mode(state: PlanState) -> bool:
       mode = state.get("config", {}).get("mode", "code")
       return mode in {"creative", "joke"}

   def creative_form_id(state: PlanState) -> str | None:
       if not is_creative_mode(state):
           return None
       form = state.get("config", {}).get("form")
       if isinstance(form, str) and form.strip():
           return form.strip()
       if state.get("config", {}).get("mode") == "joke":
           return "joke"
       return None

   def is_prose_mode(state: PlanState) -> bool:
       """True for any mode that writes a prose artifact (doc, metaplan-as-doc, creative)."""
       mode = state.get("config", {}).get("mode", "code")
       return mode in {"doc", "joke", "creative"}
   ```
2. **Re-export** from `megaplan/_core/__init__.py` so existing call sites can `from megaplan._core import is_creative_mode, creative_form_id, is_prose_mode`.

### Step 2: Create the forms registry (`megaplan/forms/__init__.py`, `megaplan/forms/joke.py`, `megaplan/forms/poem.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

   - `ProvocationCatalog` with `cuts`, `forces`, `sparks` tuples.

### Step 4: Mode-aware workflow override (`megaplan/_core/workflow.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Audit** `megaplan/_core/workflow.py:34-105`. Verified facts:
   - `WORKFLOW[STATE_INITIALIZED] = [Transition("prep", STATE_PREPPED)]` — natural path runs prep.
   - `_ROBUSTNESS_OVERRIDES["standard"]` injects `STATE_INITIALIZED → plan` (skips prep).
   - `_ROBUSTNESS_OVERRIDES["light"]` overrides INITIALIZED/CRITIQUED/EXECUTED but **not STATE_PLANNED**, so light still runs critique inherited from `WORKFLOW`.
2. **Add** a `_resolve_overrides(robustness: str, *, creative: bool) -> dict[str, list[Transition]]` helper inside workflow.py:
   - For `creative=False`: return today's `_ROBUSTNESS_OVERRIDES[robustness]` after walking `_ROBUSTNESS_WORKFLOW_LEVELS` (no behavior change).
   - For `creative=True`:
     - **standard** → return `{}` (drop the standard override that skips prep). Result: creative-standard follows `WORKFLOW[STATE_INITIALIZED] = prep → STATE_PREPPED → plan` so prep runs.
     - **light** → return `{STATE_PLANNED: [Transition("finalize", STATE_GATED)], STATE_CRITIQUED: [Transition("revise", STATE_GATED)], STATE_EXECUTED: []}`. The new STATE_PLANNED override skips critique entirely (jumping straight to finalize, then to execute via the existing STATE_GATED → finalize → STATE_FINALIZED → execute path); the inherited STATE_CRITIQUED/EXECUTED entries stay so the workflow is internally consistent if anything tries to re-enter. Importantly: **do not** inherit standard's `STATE_INITIALIZED → plan` override, so light still runs prep (1 cut + 1 spark per the brief).
     - **robust** / **superrobust** → return `{}` (no override; full critique cycles).
     - **tiny** → unchanged.
3. **Wire** `workflow_next()` (or whatever currently consults `_ROBUSTNESS_OVERRIDES[robustness]`) to call `_resolve_overrides(robustness, creative=is_creative_mode(state))` instead. Code-mode behavior is unchanged.
4. **Resolves FLAG-001 / issue_hints-1 / FLAG-005 / issue_hints.**

## Phase 2: Mode-aware sweep (replace `mode in {"doc", "joke"}` and `mode == "joke"` with helpers)

## Phase 3: Provocation engine + stance + stop-affordance

### Step 8: Provocation selection (`megaplan/forms/provocations.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Add** `select_provocations(form, *, robustness, iteration, draft_state_tags, prior_provocation_ids) -> tuple[Provocation, ...]`:
   - `light`: `(1 cut, 1 spark)`.
   - `standard`: `(1 cut, 1 force, 1 spark)`. On critique iteration N>1, exclude `prior_provocation_ids`.
   - `robust` / `superrobust`: `(1 cut, 1 force, 1 spark)` per cycle, biased by the cycle's voice's `vector_bias`.
2. **Add** `select_provocateur_voice(form, iteration) -> ProvocateurVoice | None`: rotates `formalist → iconoclast → audience` for `robust`/`superrobust`; returns `None` for light/standard.
3. **Add** `_draft_state_tags(state, plan_dir) -> tuple[str, ...]`: heuristics on the latest plan markdown. Tag `over_explained`, `safe`, `bloated`, `replaceable`. Pure, deterministic.
4. **Add** `select_active_checks(state, robustness) -> tuple[CritiqueCheckSpec, ...]`: dispatcher that wraps `select_provocations` for the active form (or falls back to `checks_for_robustness(robustness)` when `not is_creative_mode(state)`). Reads prior provocation IDs from `directors_notes.json` if it exists.

### Step 9: Critique + revise prompt builders (`megaplan/prompts/critique_creative.py`, `megaplan/prompts/revise_creative.py`)
**Scope:** Large

1. **Add** `megaplan/prompts/critique_creative.py`: reads `form = get_form(creative_form_id(state))`; calls `select_active_checks(state, robustness)`; renders chosen voice persona + (1 cut, 1 force, 1 spark) named; instructs commit-to-one-concrete-proposal-per-provocation; on standard iteration N>1 forbids relitigating prior provocation IDs; output template uses existing `findings`/`flags` shape with `FLAG-<provocation-id>`.
2. **Add** `megaplan/prompts/revise_creative.py`: mirrors `revise_joke.py`; reads `form.beat_ids` to render the canvas (poem renders `opening_image / turn / close`); standard-mode revise stance must explicitly contrast the prior stance.
3. **Update** `megaplan/audits/robustness.py`: reimplement `joke_checks_for_robustness(robustness)` to call `select_active_checks` with a synthetic creative-joke state; add `creative_checks_for_robustness(form, robustness)` exported alongside.
4. **Update** `megaplan/prompts/__init__.py`: route `is_creative_mode(state)` through the creative builders; keep joke shims.

### Step 10: Stance validator + execute prompt builder (`megaplan/forms/stance.py`, `megaplan/prompts/execute_creative.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Add** `megaplan/forms/stance.py`:
   - `STANCE_HEDGING_VERBS = ("considered","tried","attempted","sought to","aimed to","worked to")`.
   - `STANCE_CLAIM_MARKERS = ("because","refused","chose","killed","kept","fought","insisted","picked")`.
   - `validate_stance(stance: dict) -> list[str]` — returns violations. Pure function.
2. **Add** `megaplan/prompts/execute_creative.py`: generalizes `execute_joke.py`; reads `form.beat_ids` for `sections_written`; reads `form.stance_voice_hint`; stance instructions in requirements block; stop-affordance instructions (executor MAY set `stop_signal.requested=true` with a `defense`). Code mode never sets this.
3. **Refactor** `megaplan/prompts/execute_joke.py` to a shim: `_execute_joke_prompt = partial(_execute_creative_prompt, form=get_form("joke"))`.
4. **Update** `megaplan/prompts/__init__.py:128` to dispatch to `_execute_creative_prompt` for `is_creative_mode(state)`.

### Step 11: Stop-affordance at the rework branch (`megaplan/handlers/review.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Audit** `_resolve_review_outcome()` at `megaplan/handlers/review.py:149-201`. Verified call sites at lines `332-337` and `483-488` pass `(review_verdict, verdict_count, total_tasks, check_count, total_checks, missing_evidence, robustness, state, issues, criteria=…)` — no `plan_dir`.
2. **Add `plan_dir: Path` as the first positional parameter** to `_resolve_review_outcome()`. Update both call sites at `review.py:332-337` and `483-488` to pass `plan_dir` (already in scope at both call sites — both functions have `plan_dir` as a local). **(Resolves correctness-2 / callers.)**
3. **Add** stop-signal honoring on the rework branch (between the existing `rework_requested` check and the cap-key lookup at line 175):
   ```python
   if rework_requested and is_creative_mode(state):
       stop_data = _maker_requested_stop(plan_dir)
       if stop_data is not None:
           _record_maker_stop(state, plan_dir, defense=stop_data["defense"])
           return "success", STATE_DONE, None
   ```
4. **Add** helpers in the same file:
   - `_maker_requested_stop(plan_dir: Path) -> dict | None`: reads `plan_dir/finalize.json`; returns `{"defense": "..."}` if any task has `stop_signal.requested == true`, else `None`. Reads `stop_signal` from `finalize.json` because Step 5 + Step 6 propagate it there.
   - `_record_maker_stop(state, plan_dir, defense)`: writes a `meta.notes` entry and updates `directors_notes.json`'s last `passes[]` entry with `stop_requested=true` + `stop_defense=defense`.
5. **Update existing tests** that exercise `_resolve_review_outcome()` (search `tests/` for direct calls) to pass a `plan_dir` argument — typically a `tmp_path` fixture.
6. **Resolves FLAG-002 / issue_hints-2 / correctness-2 / callers / FLAG-004 (data-path consumer half).**

### Step 12: Director's notes assembled at the aggregate boundary only (`megaplan/execute/core.py`, `megaplan/forms/directors_notes.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Add** `megaplan/forms/directors_notes.py`:
   - `update_directors_notes_at_aggregate(plan_dir, state, aggregate_payload, *, iteration, voice, fired_provocations) -> None`: appends a single `passes[]` entry derived from the aggregate payload's `task_updates[].stance` and `stop_signal` fields. Reads/writes `plan_dir/directors_notes.json` atomically. Uses `iteration` as an idempotency key — if a `passes[]` entry already exists with that iteration number, it is *replaced*, not appended. **(Resolves scope.)**
2. **Hook** ONLY into `_build_aggregate_execution_payload()` at `megaplan/execute/core.py` (call sites at lines `431, 679, 1033`): when `is_creative_mode(state)`, call `update_directors_notes_at_aggregate(...)` once per execute run with the final aggregate. **Do NOT hook into `_merge_batch_results()`** — that function only propagates `stance` / `stop_signal` into `finalize.json` (Step 6) and never touches the sidecar.
3. **Hook** into `handle_critique()` so the *initial* triad is recorded in `directors_notes.json` immediately after critique writes its output, even when execution hasn't run yet. Use the same idempotency-key-by-iteration semantics — re-running critique on the same iteration replaces, doesn't append.
4. **Resolves scope-2 (single hook) and scope (idempotency).**

### Step 13: Stance validation enforcement (`megaplan/execute/core.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Update** `_merge_batch_results()` (`megaplan/execute/core.py`) to call `validate_stance(task_update["stance"])` for each task update with a stance, when `is_creative_mode(state)`. Violations are stored on the task as `stance_violations: list[str]` (a new field added to `merge_fields` in Step 6 if needed; otherwise written through to the merged task record directly).
2. **Bubble** violations into the next critique pass: the critique prompt builder (Step 9) reads `directors_notes.json` and, if the prior pass had `stance_violations`, prepends a stance-authenticity sub-provocation: "Read it aloud. If it's safe, the work is too. Rewrite the part that would embarrass you to say to a stranger."
3. **Soft enforcement only** (no hard reject).
